Clause 11.1.1 — There is a requirement to be able to address communications to a ‘functional number’

Citation: EIRENE SRS v16.0.0, clause 11.1.1, PDF p.151

11.1.1 There is a requirement to be able to address communications to a ‘functional number’ rather than a more permanent subscriber number. Such numbers are generally only associated with a user for a limited period of time. (I)

Clause 11.1.2 — This is an important issue which will affect interoperability and the ability to use

Citation: EIRENE SRS v16.0.0, clause 11.1.2, PDF p.151

11.1.2 This is an important issue which will affect interoperability and the ability to use public network services. There are specific features of individual railways which make it difficult to develop a concise and universally acceptable system within GSM. (I)

Clause 11.1.3 — To accommodate the different requirements of the individual railways, the following

Citation: EIRENE SRS v16.0.0, clause 11.1.3, PDF p.151

11.1.3 To accommodate the different requirements of the individual railways, the following approach has been adopted: (I)  all responsibility for handling addressing lies with the network infrastructure and other ground based equipment, rather than additional functionality in the mobile;  each railway will be responsible for implementing addressing schemes which best meet its needs;  national addressing schemes are to use the internationally standardised ground- train protocol, based on a single standardised GSM service, for exchanging information between the ground and mobiles.

Clause 11.2 — Ground-train addressing

Citation: EIRENE SRS v16.0.0, clause 11.2, PDF p.151

Clause 11.2.1 — The ground-train addressing can be divided into two areas: (I)

Citation: EIRENE SRS v16.0.0, clause 11.2.1, PDF p.151

11.2.1 The ground-train addressing can be divided into two areas: (I)  functional addressing of mobile users;  location dependent addressing of fixed network users.

Clause 11.2.2 — The first is related to passing information to provide an association between a mobile’s

Citation: EIRENE SRS v16.0.0, clause 11.2.2, PDF p.151

11.2.2 The first is related to passing information to provide an association between a mobile’s subscriber number and its functional number. The latter is concerned with ensuring that calls from a mobile terminal (in particular, Cab radios) are routed to the correct destination (ie primary controller, secondary controller), based on the current location of the mobile. (I)

Clause 13.1.1 — This section covers the use of the EIRENE radio system for Railway emergency calls.

Citation: EIRENE SRS v16.0.0, clause 13.1.1, PDF p.161

13.1.1 This section covers the use of the EIRENE radio system for Railway emergency calls. The section also discusses the facility for confirmation of such emergency calls and the storage of confirmation for post-incident analysis. (I)

Clause 13.1.2 — A Railway emergency call is a group call for warning drivers, controllers and other

Citation: EIRENE SRS v16.0.0, clause 13.1.2, PDF p.161

13.1.2 A Railway emergency call is a group call for warning drivers, controllers and other concerned personnel of a dangerous situation in a pre-defined area. Two types of Railway emergency calls are defined: (I) - Train emergency calls (for Railway emergencies whilst not involved in Shunting operations); - Shunting emergency calls (for Railway emergencies whilst involved in Shunting operations).

Citation: EIRENE SRS v16.0.0, clause 13.1.3, PDF p.161

13.6 Enhancement of Railway emergency calls

Clause 13.6.1 — Railway Emergency Call functionality can be improved using enhanced Railway

Citation: EIRENE SRS v16.0.0, clause 13.6.1, PDF p.163

13.6.1 Railway Emergency Call functionality can be improved using enhanced Railway Emergency Call (eREC). (I)

Clause 13.6.2 — eREC implementation shall comply with the requirements in section 13A. (M)

Citation: EIRENE SRS v16.0.0, clause 13.6.2, PDF p.163

13.6.2 eREC implementation shall comply with the requirements in section 13A. (M) UIC CODE 951-16.0.0 Page 161 PAGE LEFT INTENTIONALLY BLANK UIC CODE 951-16.0.0 Page 162 13A Enhanced Railway emergency calls 13 Railway emergency calls 13A.1 Introduction 13A.1.1 National railway operational rules regarding handling of Railway emergency calls involve safety procedures which frequently result in reducing train speed, or even stopping all trains upon reception of REC. (I) 13A.1.2 The current behaviour of VGCS does not allow discriminating between calls with the same group ID in a given cell. This may result in some specific locations (dense areas, junctions…) in broadcasting calls to a geographical area which may not fit to the strict operational requirements. This behaviour in the context of Railway emergency call causes unnecessary production loss. (I) 13A.1.3 eREC provides discrimination of Railway emergency calls in a given cell. This allows a system configuration fitting operational requirements and reducing production loss. (I) 13A.1.4 The following chapters describe the general concept, define the related functions and procedures, and specify the system requirements for eREC. (I) 13A.1.5 An eREC capable network and an eREC capable mobile shall be compliant with [eREC SPECIFICATIONS]. (M) 13A.2 Backwards compatibility 13A.2.1 An eREC capable network shall be able to simultaneously provide service to eREC and non-eREC capable mobiles. (MI) 13A.2.2 An eREC capable mobile shall be able to operate in either eREC or non-eREC capable network. (MI) 13A.3 eREC system description 13A.3.1 eREC Sector 13A.3.1.1 eREC introduces the concept of Sector. A Sector is an area defined according to railway operational needs (e.g. a given line or track), and is independent of the radio coverage area (i.e. a single cell may comprise multiple Sectors). A single eREC Sector Identity is assigned to each Sector. (I) 13A.3.1.2 An eREC Sector Identity ranges from 1 to 9. (M) 13A.3.1.3 The eREC Sector Identity is an attribute associated with a Railway Emergency Call to provide discrimination to users in a given area. (I) UIC CODE 951-16.0.0 Page 163 13A.3.2 eREC Registration/Deregistration 13A.3.2.1 An eREC capable mobile shall subscribe to eREC service by applying the eREC registration procedure. Once registered, the network shall be able to provide service to the mobile and the mobile shall be able to receive Sector Identity updates. (M) 13A.3.2.2 An eREC capable mobile shall unsubscribe from eREC service by applying the eREC deregistration procedure. (M) 13A.3.2.3 eREC management data shall be included in the functional number registration and deregistration sequences defined in paragraph 11.3.7. (M) 13A.3.3 eREC Sector Identity Update procedure 13A.3.3.1 An eREC capable mobile shall support the following means to receive Sector Identity updates: - over the air interface using eREC specific USSD messaging (M) (1)(1) - via Human Machine Interface of the mobile (M) - via balises (O) (1) the eREC Sector Identity itself is obtained from a train location information database. This interface is beyond the scope of this specification. 13A.3.3.2 An eREC capable mobile shall be informed about the Sector Identity Update method(s) supported by the network at eREC service registration. (M) 13A.3.3.3 The eREC Sector Identity Update procedure over the air interface may be supported by an eREC capable network. (O) 13A.3.4 eREC Sector Identity Validation 13A.3.4.1 The eREC Sector Identity Validation is used to detect potential misconfiguration of the Sector Identity(ies) used by an eREC capable mobile. It relies on a cell-based network configuration providing (if and where necessary) all valid eREC Sector Identities. (I) 13A.3.4.2 The eREC Sector Identity Validation Method may be supported by an eREC capable network. (O) 13A.3.4.3 The eREC Sector Identity Validation Method shall be supported by an eREC capable mobile. (M) Page 164 UIC CODE 951-16.0.0 13 Railway emergency calls 13A.3.4.4 SMS Cell Broadcast (SMS-CB) shall be used as a bearer for eREC Sector Identity Validation. (M) 13A.3.5 eREC call handling 13A.3.5.1 eREC shall use VGCS as a basis with the existing 3-digit Group ID. (M) 13A.3.5.2 The eREC Sector Identity shall be conveyed on the radio interface as part of existing call signalling: - the Sector Identity shall be prepended to the Group Id in the eREC capable mobile call setup. (M) - the Sector Identity shall be transmitted as the last digit of the Group Call Area. (M) 13A.3.5.3 The Group Call Register of an eREC capable network shall support configuration of eREC service using eREC Sector Identities. (M) 13A.3.5.4 eREC call management shall be based on the following main requirements: - Call initiation: depending on service configuration, eREC capable mobile shall provide the appropriate eREC Sector Identity at call setup. (M) - Network call establishment: eREC capable network shall establish and notify the call using the eREC Sector Identity as configured in the Group Call Register. (M) - Call reception: eREC capable mobile shall join the call if the Sector Identity in the notification messages matches an active eREC Sector Identity. (M) 13A.3.5.5 The interface between the eREC capable network and the dispatcher equipment shall be unchanged: procedures for call initiation, reception and termination are identical to REC operation and based on Group Call Register configuration. (M) 13A.3.6 Confirmation of enhanced Railway Emergency Calls 13A.3.6.1 Confirmation of High Priority Calls shall support specific data for eREC for both eREC capable mobile and network. (M) UIC CODE 951-16.0.0 Page 165 PAGE LEFT INTENTIONALLY BLANK UIC CODE 951-16.0.0 Page 166 14 Shunting mode

Citation: EIRENE SRS v16.0.0, clause 14.7.6, PDF p.173

14.7.6 The link assurance signal is a means to provide end-to-end verification between shunting users that a voice communication link is intact. Clause 14.7.10 — The link assurance signal shall consist of an intermittent audio tone (820Hz +/- 5Hz).

Citation: EIRENE SRS v16.0.0, clause 14.7.10, PDF p.174

Clause 14.7.13 — It should be possible, using maintenance facilities, to switch between the following

Citation: EIRENE SRS v16.0.0, clause 14.7.13, PDF p.174

14.7.13 It should be possible, using maintenance facilities, to switch between the following Link Assurance Signal operating modes: (O)  LAS Operating Mode 1: In order to transmit the link assurance tone, the participant must continuously hold a button depressed. If the button is released, the link assurance service will stop.  LAS Operating Mode 2: The participant transmitting the link assurance tone must confirm the transmission of that tone by periodically pressing a button (for example the PTT or LAS button). If the tone is not confirmed within a configurable time period, the link assurance service will stop (dead man’s function).  LAS Operating Mode 3: The participant transmitting the link assurance tone does not need to confirm the transmission of the tone. The link assurance signal will continue until the service is deliberately stopped (no dead man’s function).

Clause 16.1.1 — This section identifies the system requirements for the ETCS data only radio. It defines

Citation: EIRENE SRS v16.0.0, clause 16.1.1, PDF p.185

16.1.1 This section identifies the system requirements for the ETCS data only radio. It defines how the functionality is to be provided by a data radio that is dedicated to ETCS communications. (I)

Clause 16.2.1 — Figure 16-1 shows the logical architecture of an ETCS data only radio. The architecture

Citation: EIRENE SRS v16.0.0, clause 16.2.1, PDF p.185

16.2.1 Figure 16-1 shows the logical architecture of an ETCS data only radio. The architecture comprises the following elements: (I) a) At least two GSM Mobile Terminations (GSM-MT) in active/stand-by configuration: comprising one or more GSM mobile equipment and SIM cards; b) Bearer services; c) Air interface U (1) m d) ETCS data interface I (2) GSM e) There should be no MMI, maintenance indications may be provided. Figure 16-1: Logical ETCS data only radio architecture and interfaces

Clause 16.2.2 — The architecture shall comprise a number of interfaces between the different EIRENE-

Citation: EIRENE SRS v16.0.0, clause 16.2.2, PDF p.185

16.2.2 The architecture shall comprise a number of interfaces between the different EIRENE- MS elements. These are: (MI) 1) GSM-MT air interface (Um): mandatory for interoperability and conformance to GSM specifications. 2) GSM-MT – Train-borne ETCS interface (I ): specified to allow the control of GSM GSM-MT by the train-borne ETCS system, following FFFIS for EURORADIO specification [MORANE EURO FFFIS]. UIC CODE 951-16.0.0 Page 183

Clause 16.3.1 — The ETCS data only radio is a key component of the end to end ETCS system to fulfil

Citation: EIRENE SRS v16.0.0, clause 16.3.1, PDF p.186

16.3.1 The ETCS data only radio is a key component of the end to end ETCS system to fulfil the necessary QoS requirements specified in [Subset-093]. (I)

Clause 16.3.2 — The ETCS data only radio shall be capable to be operated in CS-mode as well as in PS-

Citation: EIRENE SRS v16.0.0, clause 16.3.2, PDF p.186

16.3.2 The ETCS data only radio shall be capable to be operated in CS-mode as well as in PS- mode. (MI)

Clause 16.3.3 — The ETCS data only radio shall be capable to handle at least two simultaneous ETCS

Citation: EIRENE SRS v16.0.0, clause 16.3.3, PDF p.186

16.3.3 The ETCS data only radio shall be capable to handle at least two simultaneous ETCS connections, regardless of whether these connections are established in CS or PS mode. (MI) 16.3.3.i The GSM-R PS-mode shall comprise the support of GPRS and EGPRS bearer services including all available (Modulation) Coding Schemes. (MI) 16.3.3ii The GPRS/EGPRS bearer service shall be operated considering Mobile Class A or Mobile Class B. (MI) 16.3.3iii GPRS/EGPRS capable Mobiles shall support the processing of IPv4. (MI) 16.3.3iv Cell reselection is processed by the ETCS data only radio autonomously. (I) 16.3.3v Network Assisted Cell Change (NACC) by the network as well as by the EDOR shall be according to [ETSI TS 103 328]. (M) 16.3.3vi The discontinuation of the data flow using GPRS/EGPRS bearer service shall be less than:  1,5 s in 95% of cases (M)  2 s in 99% of cases (M) 16.3.3vii The ETCS data only radio in PS-mode shall support the parameters of the QoS profile “ETCS application” according [FFFIS for EuroRadio]. (MI) 16.3.3viii The EDOR in PS-mode should be used for other signalling applications simultaneously to ETCS (e.g. KMS). (I) 16.3.3ix To allow the concurrent operation of different data communication flows that comprises the use of different APNs, the ETCS data only radio shall support at least “4” primary PDP contexts. (M) 16.3.3x For the operation of non-ETCS applications, the ETCS data only radio in PS-mode shall support the parameters of the QoS profile in 10.8.5.1. (M) 16.3.3xi For the operation of multiple PDP contexts, the EDOR shall support multiple virtual interfaces on the R-reference point according to [MORANE EURO FFFIS]. (M) Page 184 UIC CODE 951-16.0.0 16 ETCS data only radio

16.3.4 All GSM-MT of the ETCS data only radio shall be initialised and controlled by the ETCS train-borne system according to FFFIS for EURORADIO specification [MORANE EURO FFFIS]. (MI)
